# House Committee on Appropriations: Budget Amendments Review Subcommittee - October 1, 2026 — Source-Linked Digest

Source: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=iFy55qIC4yk

AI-assisted working draft. AI can make errors. Always verify consequential interpretations and wording against the cited transcript and official video.

## At a glance

- **Education and access:** The committee considered school safety, special-education support, tutoring, facilities, scholarships, and technical training proposals.

- **Agriculture and implementation:** Members discussed agricultural production and infrastructure, alongside special provisions intended to address implementation and service-delivery needs.

- **Fiscal constraints:** Members described limited fiscal space and left additional agency requests and provisions for further consideration.

- **Committee disposition:** The chair routed the line-by-line recommendations into a proposed substitute bill for Plenary consideration.

Reading note: Agency totals pair the chair?s rounded summary with the exact amount shown on screen. Ordinary rounding does not indicate a discrepancy. PCO, DND and DHSUD retain specific transcript wording or unit problems; DILG and DSWD identify earlier and final readouts. The session total is calculated from non-overlapping displayed amounts and excludes transfers. A complete net-change total is unavailable.

## Session and agency amendment summaries

### Total amendments

The fiscal year 2027 amendments approved by the subcommittee total PHP 116,317,060,000 (about PHP 116.32 billion), calculated from 33 non-overlapping agency and department amounts shown on screen.

This total covers additions and restorations. It excludes transfers, overlapping cluster subtotals, withdrawn proposals and requests left for consideration. The hearing did not establish a complete total for deletions or offsets, so the net budget change cannot be calculated. These are subcommittee-approved proposals, subject to the remaining House budget process.

### Department of Education (DepEd) amendment summary

The chair summarized the amendments at PHP 1,952 million, matching the exact amount shown: PHP 1,952,000,000.

- School safety and security: Chair Rep. Mikaela Angela B. Suansing moved a PHP 500 million DepEd school-safety insertion; the program had zero FY2027 NEP funding. Approved.
- Special-education programs and party-list submissions: Rep. Allan U. Ty relayed party-list institutional amendments for consideration. No separate amount was specified; the approved Madrasa, special-needs and IPED amounts are listed below.
- Madrasa Education Program: Chair Suansing moved PHP 50 million for DepEd's Madrasa Education Program. Approved.
- Special Needs Education Program: Chair Suansing moved a PHP 75 million DepEd increase for the Special Needs Education Program, above its PHP 849 million NEP allocation. Approved.
- Indigenous Peoples Education Program: Chair Suansing moved PHP 25 million for IPED, whose NEP allocation was PHP 76 million. Approved.
- Academic Recovery and Accessible Learning: Chair Suansing proposed PHP 350 million for the ARAL tutoring program, which had zero FY2027 NEP funding. Approved.
- Basic Education Facilities program: Chair Suansing moved PHP 952 million for DepEd Basic Education Facilities classroom construction. Approved.
- Requested special provisions (not approved): DepEd requested school-site and subsidy eligibility changes, PHP 100.8 million for competition participation, expanded school-feeding beneficiaries, school-security authority, and PHP 6.61 billion for ARAL learning recovery. These requests were not carried amendments.

### Commission on Higher Education (CHED) amendment summary

The chair summarized the amendments at about PHP 3.87 billion; the exact amount shown was PHP 3,868,865,000.

- Tertiary Education Subsidy: Senior Deputy Speaker Ferdinand L. Hernandez moved PHP 2,102,475,000 for CHED's Tertiary Education Subsidy, including 4Ps beneficiaries and the stated 2026-28 coverage periods. Approved.
- Tulong Dunong Program: Senior Deputy Speaker Hernandez moved PHP 1,766,390,000 for CHED's Tulong Dunong Program, which had zero FY2027 NEP funding. Approved.
- Requested special provisions (not approved): CHED requested authority to use higher-education development income for funding deficiencies and restoration of a 2026 provision. Not approved in this unit. No separate peso amount was specified.

### Legal Education Board (LEB) amendment summary

Requests for consideration; no approved amendment total.

- Clinical Legal Education Program request: Rep. Johanne Monich G. Bautista, on behalf of Rep. Renee Louise M. Co, submitted LEB's PHP 57 million CLEP request for consideration; no approval is recorded.
- Legal Education Access and Development Scholarship: Rep. Brian Poe submitted a roughly PHP 83 million scholarship request for consideration; no approval is recorded.

### University of the Philippines (UP) System amendment summary

The chair summarized the amendments at PHP 360 million, matching the exact amount shown: PHP 360,000,000.

- UP Center for Education and Development proposal: Deputy Speaker Janette L. Garin moved an additional PHP 70 million for the UP Center for Education and Development program supporting EDCOM 2 and CAPE; CAPE had no FY2027 NEP funding. Approved.
- Project NOAH increase: Rep. Brian Poe moved PHP 275 million for Project NOAH research services at the UP Resilience Institute. Approved.
- UP Diliman Institute of Mathematics addition: Rep. Brian Poe moved PHP 15 million for UP Diliman's Institute of Mathematics, which had zero FY2027 NEP funding. Approved.

### State Universities and Colleges (SUCs) amendment summary

The chair summarized the amendments at PHP 506 million, matching the exact amount shown: PHP 506,000,000.

- Locally funded projects for various state universities and colleges: Rep. Stephen James T. Tan moved PHP 456 million for FY2027 locally funded SUC projects; after correcting the initial figure, the motion carried.
- State universities and colleges: Rep. Stephen James T. Tan later added PHP 50 million to the prior PHP 456 million SUC amount, bringing the carried BARSC total to PHP 506 million.

### Technical Education and Skills Development Authority (TESDA) amendment summary

The chair summarized the amendments at about PHP 1.44 billion; the exact amount shown was PHP 1,435,910,000.

- Training for Work Scholarship Program: Rep. Kristine Singson-Meehan moved a PHP 966,650,000 increase for TESDA's Training for Work Scholarship Program. Approved.
- Special Training for Employment Program: Rep. Kristine Singson-Meehan moved a PHP 469,260,000 increase for TESDA's Special Training for Employment Program. Approved.
- Technical Education and Skills Development Authority: TESDA requested a Sariling Sika Program provision on use of its revolving fund and operating-unit income; disposition is not recorded here. No separate peso amount was specified.

### Department of Agriculture (DA) amendment summary

The chair summarized the amendments at about PHP 16.44 billion; the exact amount shown was PHP 16,440,224,000. This corrected the earlier PHP 15.4 billion readout.

- Expanded fertilizer assistance program: Rep. Wilfrido Mark M. Enverga moved PHP 500 million for DA's expanded fertilizer-assistance program, above its stated PHP 14,443,499,000 NEP allocation. Approved.
- High-value crops and ube: Rep. Brian Poe withdrew his earlier PHP 100 million fertilizer-to-ube realignment. Rep. Enverga then moved one PHP 100 million high-value-crops increase, with the ube earmark within that amount; approved.
- Solar pumps for irrigation: Rep. Enverga moved PHP 125 million for DA solar irrigation pumps outside NIA service areas. Approved.
- Hybrid solar-powered ice plants: Rep. Enverga moved PHP 105 million for BFAR hybrid solar-powered ice plants; island-fishing areas were proposed as priority sites. Approved.
- Agri-transport and tram system: Rep. Enverga moved PHP 150 million for an agri-transport cable-car or tram project; it had zero FY2027 NEP allocation. Approved.
- Farm-to-market roads: Rep. Enverga moved an additional PHP 4,960,224,000 for farm-to-market roads, citing gaps despite PHP 16 billion already available. Approved.
- Fish-port construction, rehabilitation and improvement: Rep. Enverga moved PHP 500 million for PFDA fish-port construction, rehabilitation and improvement, above its stated PHP 2.1 billion NEP budget. Approved.
- Presidential Assistance to Farmers and Fisher Folk: Chair Suansing, on behalf of Speaker Bojie Dy, moved to restore PHP 10 billion for Presidential Assistance to Farmers and Fisher Folk (PAF), which had zero FY2027 NEP funding. Approved.
- PAF access, eligibility and delivery: Deputy Speaker Yevgeny Vincente B. Emano asked DA to extend PAF access to farm workers; Rep. Enverga proposed including FishR registrants. Chair Suansing asked DA to review eligibility and records; no separate priced motion was carried. No separate peso amount was specified.
- Requested special provisions (not approved): DA requested direct Quick Response Fund equipment authority, restoration of a presidential-assistance provision, and PHP 51 million for citizen monitoring through the Philippine Council for Agriculture and Fisheries. Not approved in this unit.

### Department of Agrarian Reform (DAR) amendment summary

The chair summarized the amendments at PHP 331 million, matching the exact amount shown: PHP 331,000,000.

- Climate Resilience Farm Productivity Support: Deputy Speaker Janette L. Garin moved PHP 331 million for DAR's Climate Resilience Farm Productivity Support program. Approved.
- Department of Agrarian Reform: The Committee Secretary described a DAR special-provision update as conforming to amounts already approved, not a new amendment. No separate peso amount was specified.

### National Irrigation Administration (NIA) amendment summary

The chair summarized the amendments at about PHP 4.09 billion; the exact amount shown was PHP 4,092,500,000.

- National Irrigation System (NIS): Rep. Allan U. Ty moved PHP 1,310,500,000 for NIA's National Irrigation System. Approved.
- Communal Irrigation System (CIS): Rep. Ty moved PHP 147 million for NIA's Communal Irrigation System. Approved.
- Other Irrigation System Subprogram - pump irrigation repair: Rep. Ty proposed PHP 60 million for pump-irrigation repair under NIA's Other Irrigation System Subprogram. Approved.
- Establishment of Pump Irrigation Projects (EPIP): Rep. Allan U. Ty moved an additional PHP 1,175,000,000 for the Establishment of Pump Irrigation Projects. The chair announced that the amount was added.
- New National Irrigation Subprogram - Locally Funded Projects: Rep. Ty moved PHP 1 billion for NIA's new National Irrigation Subprogram under Locally Funded Projects. Approved.
- Pantingan Small Reservoir Irrigation Project: Rep. Ty moved PHP 400 million for the Pantingan Small Reservoir Irrigation Project. Approved.

### Department of Environment and Natural Resources (DENR) amendment summary

The chair summarized the amendments at about PHP 209.9 million; the exact amount shown was PHP 209,906,000. The chair clarified the earlier PHP 209 million readout.

- Land Management and Governance: Deputy Speaker Jose C. Alvarez moved PHP 59,906,000 for DENR Land Management and Governance. Approved.
- Release of unclaimed land patents: Deputy Speaker Alvarez moved PHP 100 million for DENR to release unclaimed land patents. Approved.
- Region 10 program start for structures: Deputy Speaker Alvarez moved PHP 50 million to start the DENR structures program in Region 10. Approved.
- Requested special provisions (not approved): DENR requested climate-information, nature-based planning, water-warning, habitat-protection, river-basin database, greenhouse-gas inventory and land-registration provisions, plus automatic release of mineral-reservation appropriations and the Mines and Geosciences Bureau's 10% share. No fixed additional peso amount was specified; these provisions were not approved.

### Presidential Communications Office (PCO) amendment summary

The exact amendment total shown was PHP 672,986,000. The transcript records the chair saying PHP 672.99 billion; that unit differs from the amount on screen.

- Bagong Pilipinas Studios: Rep. Brian Raymund S. Yamsuan proposed a PHP 25 million increase for PCO Proper to establish and operate Bagong Pilipinas Studios. Approved.
- IBC employee compensation subsidy: Rep. Yamsuan moved an additional PHP 35,985,750 for IBC employee compensation. Approved; the final schedule rounded this to PHP 35,986,000.
- Temporary PIA offices during building renovation: Rep. Yamsuan moved PHP 12 million for temporary Philippine Information Agency office rental during building renovation. Approved.
- Restoration and strengthening of public-service media capacity: Rep. Yamsuan moved PHP 600 million to restore and strengthen People's Television Network public-service media capacity. Approved.

### Energy Regulatory Commission (ERC) amendment summary

The chair summarized the amendments at PHP 75 million, matching the exact amount shown: PHP 75,000,000.

- Original ERC request and refinement: Rep. Jurdin Jesus "JJ" M. Romualdo withdrew his earlier PHP 227 million ERC motion and appealed for PHP 75 million if funds remained. That conditional appeal was not separately voted on; the three approved PHP 25 million items are listed below.
- ERC consulting services - first regulatory period, third entry group: Rep. Arnie B. Fuentebella moved PHP 25 million for ERC consulting services on the third entry group regulatory period. Approved.
- ERC consulting services - first regulatory period, fourth entry group: Rep. Fuentebella moved PHP 25 million for ERC consulting services on the fourth entry group regulatory period. Approved.
- ERC consulting services - NGCP sixth regulatory period: Rep. Fuentebella moved PHP 25 million for ERC consulting services on NGCP's sixth regulatory period. Approved.

### Department of the Interior and Local Government (DILG) amendment summary

The final amendment total was PHP 478,000,000, matching the chair's final summary of PHP 478 million. An earlier readout was PHP 478.8 million.

- NAPOLCOM independent police oversight: Rep. Arnie B. Fuentebella moved PHP 10 million for NAPOLCOM confidential and intelligence funds supporting independent police oversight. Approved.
- PNP Counterterrorism Center: Rep. Arnie B. Fuentebella sought PHP 500 million for the PNP Counterterrorism Center and advocated PHP 200 million during discussion. His final PHP 100 million motion for initial funding was approved.
- Negros Island Region headquarters and facilities proposal: Rep. Fuentebella moved PHP 100 million in initial funding for two camps in the new Negros Island Region. Approved; the displayed line identifies regional headquarters and other facilities for PRO Negros Island Region.
- BFP firetruck procurement: Rep. Fuentebella moved PHP 235 million for BFP firetruck procurement. Approved.
- BFP ambulance procurement: Rep. Fuentebella moved PHP 18 million for BFP ambulance procurement. Approved.
- BFP search-and-rescue vehicle procurement: Rep. Fuentebella moved PHP 15 million for BFP search-and-rescue vehicles. Approved.
- Internal Affairs Service special-provision amendments: Rep. Allan U. Ty submitted IAS special-provision amendments for inclusion; no specific provision or disposition is recorded here. No separate peso amount was specified.
- NAPOLCOM rental-income special provision: Rep. Fuentebella proposed allowing NAPOLCOM to retain and use rental income for operating expenses, capital outlay and MOOE; text was awaited, with no approval recorded. No separate peso amount was specified.
- Requested special provisions (not approved): Requests sought NAPOLCOM authority to use collected rental income and a new provision for PNP-IAS operating requirements; not approved in this unit. No separate peso amount was specified.

### Department of Transportation (DOTr) amendment summary

The chair summarized the amendments at about PHP 2.35 billion; the exact amount shown was PHP 2,349,000,000.

- Service Contracting Program: Rep. Edwin Ongchuan moved PHP 500 million for DOTr's Service Contracting Program, which had zero FY2027 NEP funding. Approved.
- Fuel subsidy to the transport sector: Rep. Ongchuan moved PHP 1 billion for a transport-sector fuel subsidy, which had zero NEP allocation. Approved.
- NCR Busway Project: Rep. Ongchuan moved PHP 75 million for the NCR Busway Project, whose FY2027 NEP allocation was zero; the agency request was PHP 1.756 billion. Approved.
- Locally funded project: Rep. Ongchuan presented DOTr's PHP 774 million locally funded project proposal. Approved.
- Requested special provisions (not approved): DOTr requested intermodal and multimodal service-contracting and transport fuel-subsidy provisions; unpriced and not approved here.

### National Poverty Alleviation Commission (NAPC) amendment summary

The chair summarized the amendments at PHP 100 million, matching the exact amount shown: PHP 100,000,000.

- NAPC MOOE appropriation: Minority Leader Marcelino C. Libanan moved an additional PHP 100 million for general NAPC maintenance and operating expenses. Approved. NAPC Chairperson Manny Pacquiao separately requested PHP 10 billion for Masaganang Pamumuhay sa Bagong Pilipinas; that program request remained for consideration.

### Department of Justice (DOJ) amendment summary

The chair summarized the amendments at about PHP 132 million; the exact amount shown was PHP 132,050,000.

- Digitalization program: Rep. Amado Carlos A. Bolilia IV moved an additional PHP 16 million for the Office of the Solicitor General digitalization program. Approved.
- Victims' Compensation Program: Rep. Bolilia moved an additional PHP 7.8 million for DOJ's Victims' Compensation Program. Approved.
- Cybercrime program ICT software: Rep. Bolilia moved an additional PHP 18,250,000 for Office of the Secretary cybercrime ICT software, above the PHP 32,458,000 NEP amount. Approved.
- OSAEC-NCC program: Rep. Bolilia moved an additional PHP 10 million for DOJ's anti-online sexual-abuse coordination center. Approved.
- Office of the Secretary confidential fund: Rep. Bolilia moved an additional PHP 30 million for the DOJ Office of the Secretary confidential fund. Approved.
- NBI Confidential Fund augmentation: Deputy Speaker Janette L. Garin moved an additional PHP 50 million for the NBI Confidential Fund. Approved.
- Requested special provisions (not approved): DOJ requested restoration of the 2026 PCGG provision for use and recording of proceeds; unpriced and not approved here.

### Department of Trade and Industry (DTI) amendment summary

The chair summarized the amendments at PHP 80 million, matching the exact amount shown: PHP 80,000,000.

### Department of Foreign Affairs (DFA) amendment summary

The chair summarized the amendments at PHP 37.5 million, matching the exact amount shown: PHP 37,500,000.

- Diplomacy Program augmentation: Rep. Brian Poe moved an additional PHP 37.5 million for DFA's Diplomacy Program. Approved.

### Department of Social Welfare and Development (DSWD) amendment summary

The chair summarized the final amendments at about PHP 26.86 billion; the exact amount shown was PHP 26,860,300,000.

- Assistance to Individuals in Crisis Situations: Rep. JC Rahman A. Nava, M.D., moved PHP 25,806,000,000 for AICS above its PHP 33 billion FY2027 NEP amount; approved. His PHP 76 billion agency request and Libanan's alternate figures were not the carried motion.
- Protect Teen: Rep. Nava moved PHP 25 million to restore Protect Teen to its FY2026 GAA level. Approved.
- Project Aruga: Rep. Nava moved PHP 25 million for Project Aruga, matching the FY2026 GAA level he cited. Approved.
- Project SAFE: Rep. Nava moved an additional PHP 13.8 million for Project SAFE. Approved.
- Quick Response Fund ceiling within the NDRRMF: Rep. Nava moved to raise the NDRRMF Quick Response Fund ceiling from PHP 1.25 billion to PHP 3 billion, a PHP 1.75 billion ceiling transfer, not a new allocation. Approved.
- Northern Mindanao Disaster Resource and Support Center: Rep. Nava moved PHP 100 million for a disaster resource and support center in Manolo Fortich, Bukidnon; approved. The chair also urged release of the prior Caraga center funding.
- Future regional disaster-support coverage: Minority Leader Libanan requested consideration of regional disaster-support centers, including Samar; no priced motion was made.
- Sustainable Livelihood Program: Rep. Nava moved an additional PHP 870.5 million for DSWD's Sustainable Livelihood Program. Approved.
- DSWD Special Committee of LGBTQA+: Rep. Nava proposed PHP 20 million for the DSWD Special Committee of LGBTQA+, which had no FY2027 NEP allocation. Approved.
- KALAHI-CIDSS request: Rep. Nava manifested an additional PHP 20 million for KALAHI-CIDSS; the request was left for later fiscal space.
- Project LAWA at BINHI request: Rep. Nava manifested an additional PHP 1.4 billion for Project LAWA at BINHI; the request was left for later fiscal space.
- National Authority for Child Care request: Rep. Nava manifested PHP 53.7 million for regional National Authority for Child Care social-work positions; left for consideration.
- Additional social-pension coverage request: Rep. Nava requested coverage for 550,000 additional eligible people aged 70 and above; no amount was stated, and the request was left for later fiscal space.
- Requested special provisions (not approved): DSWD requested protective-services and Pamana provisions, and PHP 8 million to implement Executive Order 51. Rep. Percival "Perci" Cendaña was named as requester for the PHP 8 million provision. These requests were not approved.

### Department of Health (DOH) amendment summary

The chair summarized the amendments at about PHP 28.36 billion; the exact amount shown was PHP 28,364,776,000.

- MAIFIP increase and remaining funding gap: Rep. Albert S. Garcia moved PHP 24,965,776,000 for MAIFIP, above its PHP 24,235,078,000 FY2027 NEP allocation. Approved; the chair clarified it restored the 2026 H-GAB level, leaving an estimated PHP 2 billion gap.
- MAIFIP access and proposed special-provision wording: Deputy Speaker Janette L. Garin proposed broader MAIFIP eligibility, simpler documentation and private-room coverage for qualifying patients; the wording remained under development, with no vote recorded. No separate peso amount was specified.
- Health Facilities Enhancement Program funding increase: Rep. Albert S. Garcia moved an additional PHP 1,549,000,000 for HFEP, above its PHP 4,472,659,000 FY2027 proposed budget. Approved.
- HFEP allocation criteria special provision: Rep. JC Rahman A. Nava, M.D., proposed HFEP allocation standards by facility level; Garcia agreed to develop wording with him. No provision was adopted. No separate peso amount was specified.
- Proposed sin-tax funding provision for HFEP: Rep. Albert S. Garcia proposed a special provision to use unallocated sin-tax balances for HFEP after Treasury, DBM and DOH reconciliation; not adopted. No separate peso amount was specified.
- Nationally procured communicable-disease commodities: Rep. Albert S. Garcia moved an additional PHP 1.8 billion for DOH communicable-disease commodities, above the PHP 6,892,852,000 FY2027 budget. Approved.
- OSEC - Operations of DOH Regional Hospitals: Rep. Albert S. Garcia moved an additional PHP 50 million for DOH regional-hospital operations. Approved.
- PhilHealth funding increase request relayed by the minority: Minority Leader Marcelino C. Libanan relayed Rep. Iris Marie Demesa Montes's call for more PhilHealth funding, citing PHP 60 billion available against PHP 244 billion needed for universal health care. No specific increase was moved or approved.
- Proposed reconciliation of PhilHealth balances for special-provision access: Rep. Albert S. Garcia proposed reconciling PhilHealth balances and unallocated PCSO/PAGCOR sin-tax funds for possible special-provision access; it remained conditional on confirmed balances. No separate peso amount was specified.
- Requested special provisions (not approved): DOH requested raising HFEP overhead for projects over PHP 1 million from 1% to 2.5%, plus provisions on patient aid, health workforce, facilities, research releases, board honoraria, PhilHealth subsidies, reorganization costs and commodity-fund coordination; not approved here.

### Department of Public Works and Highways (DPWH) amendment summary

The chair summarized the amendments at PHP 11.49 billion, matching the exact amount shown: PHP 11,490,000,000.

- National Roads Program increase: Committee on Public Works chair Rep. Romeo S. Momo Sr. moved an additional PHP 6,917,778,000 for the National Roads Program. Approved.
- National Bridge Program increase: Rep. Momo moved PHP 175,874,000 for unfinished national bridges under the National Bridge Program. Approved.
- National Building Program increase: Rep. Momo moved an additional PHP 307.5 million for the National Building Program. Approved.
- Convergence and Special Support Program increase: Rep. Momo moved an additional PHP 4,088,848,000 for the Convergence and Special Support Program. Approved.
- DPWH special provision for correcting project data: Rep. Momo proposed a provision to correct printed project-listing data; it remained for consideration. No separate peso amount was specified.
- Project-detail correction provision and safeguards: Rep. Momo and the chair proposed allowing corrections to project-listing errors while prohibiting changes to project amounts; the provision remained for refinement. No separate peso amount was specified.
- Requested special provisions (not approved): DPWH requested project-detail refinement authority and restoration of the 2026 right-of-way and contractual-obligations provision. Rep. Sergio C. Dagooc was named as requester for the restoration. No additional amount was specified; the requests were not approved.

### Department of Labor and Employment (DOLE) amendment summary

The chair summarized the amendments at about PHP 5.11 billion; the exact amount shown was PHP 5,113,000,000.

- TUPAD emergency-employment increase: Rep. Johanne Monich G. Bautista moved PHP 5 billion for TUPAD, raising its stated amount from PHP 14,246,077,000 to PHP 19,246,077,000. Approved.
- Further augmentation request against the prior-year appropriation: Minority Leader Libanan requested a further DOLE increase, noting the PHP 19.2 billion proposal was PHP 2 billion below the PHP 21.2 billion FY2026 GAA level he cited; no new motion was made.
- Integrated Livelihood and Emergency Employment Program (DILEEP): Rep. Bautista moved PHP 113 million for DILEEP, raising the stated amount from PHP 2,227,092,000 to PHP 2,340,092,000. Approved.

### Local Government Support Fund (LGSF) amendment summary

The chair summarized the amendments at about PHP 4.09 billion; the exact amount shown was PHP 4,091,400,000.

- LGSF increase motion: Deputy Speaker Yevgeny Vincente B. Emano moved an additional PHP 4,091,400,000 for LGSF, whose displayed FY2027 NEP allocation was PHP 58,532,578,000. Approved. Illustrative local-government grant tiers were examples, not separate amendments.
- LGSF safeguards and proposed special provision: Minority Leader Libanan requested LGSF safeguards; the chair invited proposed wording for consideration, and Libanan undertook to submit it. No separate peso amount was specified.

### Department of National Defense (DND) amendment summary

The exact amendment total shown was PHP 553,765,000. The transcript records the chair saying PHP 553.77 billion; that unit differs from the amount on screen. The five numbered items were presented without descriptions for national-security reasons.

- DND item 1 additional allocation: Rep. Jose "Joboy" S. Aquino II moved PHP 108,765,000 for DND item 1. Approved.
- DND item 2 additional allocation: Rep. Aquino moved PHP 285 million for DND item 2. Approved.
- DND item 3 additional allocation: Rep. Aquino moved PHP 60 million for DND item 3. Approved.
- DND item 4 additional allocation: Rep. Aquino moved PHP 85 million for DND item 4. Approved.
- DND item 5 additional allocation: Rep. Aquino moved PHP 15 million for DND item 5. Approved.
- Requested special provisions (not approved): DND requested restoration of the 2026 GAA foreign- and domestic-financing agreements provision; unpriced and not approved here.

### Department of Migrant Workers (DMW) amendment summary

The chair summarized the amendments at PHP 250 million, matching the exact amount shown: PHP 250,000,000.

- AKSYON Fund for migrant-worker assistance: Rep. Johanne Monich G. Bautista moved a PHP 150 million increase for the AKSYON Fund; approved against the agency's larger PHP 1 billion request.
- OWWA Emergency Repatriation Fund increase: Rep. Bautista moved a fixed PHP 100 million increase for OWWA's Emergency Repatriation Fund; the separate PHP 575 million agency request remained for consideration. Approved.
- General Administration and Support MOOE request: Rep. Bautista submitted OWWA's additional PHP 353 million MOOE request for consideration; no approval is recorded.
- Capital outlay request: Rep. Bautista submitted OWWA's additional PHP 447 million capital-outlay request for consideration; no approval is recorded.
- Alagang OWWA request: Rep. Bautista submitted OWWA's additional PHP 433 million Alagang OWWA request for consideration; no approval is recorded.
- Congressional Migrant Workers Scholarship Program request: Rep. Bautista submitted OWWA's additional PHP 60 million scholarship-program request for consideration; no approval is recorded.
- Workers Auxiliary Support Project request: Rep. Bautista submitted OWWA's additional PHP 130 million Workers Auxiliary Support Project request for consideration; no approval is recorded.
- Requested special provisions (not approved): DMW requested restoration of 2026 GAA provisions for AKSYON Fund administration and reintegration-program monitoring; unpriced and not approved here.

## Department of Education (DepEd) amendments

### School safety and security

Suansing said DepEd, in a request signed by Secretary Sonny Angara, sought about PHP 800 million for school safety and security. She said the program had zero funding in the FY 2027 NEP. Citing school shootings, violence, and potential threats to students, she proposed a PHP 500 million insertion for school safety and security under DepEd. Senior Deputy Speaker Ferdinand L. Hernandez supported the school-safety proposal, citing a recent shooting in his district and the need to prevent further loss of life. Chairperson Rep. Mikaela Angela B. Suansing then stated that the Budget Amendments Review Subcommittee concurred and asked the Committee Secretary to enter PHP 500 million in the row. Suansing then began putting the proposal as a formal motion after a member requested it. Suansing formally moved to allocate PHP 500 million for DepEd school safety and security. The motion was duly seconded; after the chair heard no objection, she declared it carried.

Suansing grouped the Madrasa Education Program, Special Needs Education Program, and Indigenous Peoples Education Program (IPED) under DepEd, noting that the programs had received BARSC funding the prior year and were positively received. Ty said Kabataan Party List and ACT Teachers Party List had submitted institutional amendments for consideration; he thought they covered all three programs. Suansing also recalled Akbayan Party List and ACT Teachers Party List advocating additional funding for the same programs during earlier deliberations.

### Requested special provisions (not approved)

The Department of Education requested revised eligible uses for acquiring or improving school sites, and broader government assistance and subsidy coverage with cost- and capacity-based review and disclosure requirements. It also requested PHP 100.8 million for financially challenged learners, teachers, coaches, and schools participating in DepEd-endorsed or recognized competitions.

The school-based feeding request would extend beneficiaries to incoming Grade 1 pupils, pregnant or lactating adolescents enrolled in public schools with infants aged 0 to 1, and vulnerable, marginalized, or undernourished learners beyond Grade 6. A school-security provision would authorize operating units to engage security services, chargeable to school safety/security or other allowable funds subject to fund availability and procurement law.

The ARAL provision requested PHP 6.61 billion for related learning recovery, including summer learning, remediation, enrichment, camps, resources, tutors, training, monitoring, evaluation, and implementation support.

## Commission on Higher Education (CHED) amendments

### Tertiary Education Subsidy

Senior Deputy Speaker Ferdinand L. Hernandez moved for BARSC to adopt the Commission on Higher Education (CHED) PHP 2.102475 billion proposal for its Tertiary Education Subsidy, including 4Ps and covering the ongoing 2026 period, the second semester of AY 2026-27, and the first semester of AY 2027-28. He cited the program's importance to scholarship beneficiaries and said almost 400,000 new students came from the 4Ps list. Minority Leader Marcelino C. Libanan seconded; the chair declared the motion carried. In a later recap, Chairperson Rep. Mikaela Angela B. Suansing described the CHED addition as PHP 2.1 billion. The schedule displays PHP 2,102,475,000 in the BARSC Proposal column. The final chair restatement and spreadsheet retained PHP 2,102,475,000 after discussion of rounding budget entries to thousands.

### Project NOAH increase

Rep. Brian Poe requested and moved a PHP 275 million increase for research services under Project NOAH at the UP Resilience Institute, citing ongoing flooding. Chair Mikaela Angela B. Suansing noted that the House and Senate had added PHP 1 billion for Project NOAH in the 2026 budget and said its SARO had been released to UP and operations were ramping up. She described satellite mapping at 1-by-1-square-meter resolution and said she was working with Project NOAH, the UP Resilience Institute, and scientific stakeholders to vet DPWH flood-control, drainage, and related projects in the NEP for need and implementability. After a false start between 250 and 275, the chair clarified the motion as PHP 275 million toward Project NOAH; it was duly seconded and carried without objection.

### UP Diliman Institute of Mathematics addition

Poe requested PHP 15 million for the UP Diliman Institute of Mathematics, arguing that investment in mathematics would benefit the economy, and moved for its inclusion. After a brief 15-versus-50 clarification, Poe confirmed 15 million; the motion was seconded and carried without objection. Deputy Speaker Janette L. Garin noted that the Institute, recognized as a center of excellence since 1998, had zero FY 2027 NEP funding. She said the PHP 15 million would be a step toward producing more mathematics researchers, and the chair confirmed the zero NEP amount.

## State Universities and Colleges (SUCs) amendments

### Locally funded projects for various state universities and colleges

Rep. Stephen James T. Tan presented a request for state universities and colleges for PHP 456 million for FY 2027 locally funded projects for various state universities. He initially moved to approve or allocate PHP 465 million. After the chair said the projects covered various state universities and asked whether they totaled PHP 475 million, Tan corrected the figure to PHP 456 million. The chair restated PHP 456 million for locally funded projects for various state universities and colleges; the motion was seconded and carried without objection.

## Technical Education and Skills Development Authority (TESDA) amendments

### Training for Work Scholarship Program

Rep. Kristine Singson-Meehan presented TESDA's request from Secretary Kiko Benitez for additions to the Training for Work Scholarship Program (TWSP) and STEP. She said the funding would serve additional learners, expand access to technical-vocational education and training, and strengthen beneficiaries' employability and skills. TWSP courses included IT, electronics, tourism, agriculture, welding, dressmaking, barista, and caregiving, with employment as the aim. Singson-Meehan moved a PHP 966,650,000 increase for TWSP; Suansing restated it as the Training for Work Scholarship Program under TESDA, and the motion was duly seconded and carried without objection.

## Department of Agriculture (DA) amendments

### Expanded fertilizer assistance program

Rep. Wilfrido Mark M. Enverga proposed a PHP 500 million increase for the Department of Agriculture (DA) expanded fertilizer assistance program, whose 2027 NEP allocation he gave as PHP 14,443,499,000. He said the program would help sustain farmers' planting intentions and offset fertilizer costs as fuel prices rise. The motion was seconded and carried without objection.

### High-value crops and ube

Earlier, Rep. Brian Poe moved to realign PHP 100 million from the fertilizer budget to support ube farming. Enverga welcomed consideration of the idea at the appropriate time, noting ube export was among DA's priorities and requests; Poe then retracted the motion and left it as a manifestation. Later, Enverga proposed a PHP 100 million increase for the high-value crop sector, Poe described the baseline as PHP 1.7 billion and said the increase would bring the total to PHP 1.8 billion. He asked to earmark PHP 100 million for ube; Enverga agreed, and Minority Leader Marcelino C. Libanan supported the ube focus, citing demand and the Region 8 tinampay variety. The chair put one PHP 100 million addition to high-value crops to the body, and it carried without objection; the proposed ube earmark was within that increase, not an additional PHP 100 million.

### Hybrid solar-powered ice plants

For fisheries, Enverga proposed PHP 105 million for hybrid solar-powered ice plants under the Bureau of Fisheries and Aquatic Resources (BFAR), clarifying that these were ice-producing facilities rather than cold storage. Alvarez supported the increase and asked that distribution prioritize island fishing areas; Suansing suggested a pilot in each island group. The motion was seconded and carried without objection.

### Agri-transport and tram system

Enverga proposed PHP 150 million for an Agri-Transport and Support Infrastructure Project using cable-car or tram systems to move high-value crops from mountainous production areas as a lower-cost alternative to farm-to-market roads. The motion to provide PHP 150 million under DA was seconded and carried without objection.

### Presidential Assistance to Farmers and Fisher Folk

Chair Suansing, speaking on behalf of Speaker Bojie Dy, advocated restoring PHP 10 billion for Presidential Assistance to Farmers and Fisher Folk (PAF), which she said had a zero 2027 NEP allocation. She recalled that PHP 10 billion had been provided the prior year and cited the program's use to respond to farmers' needs amid higher fuel, fertilizer and other farm-input costs. Senior Deputy Speaker Ferdinand L. Hernandez urged members to continue supporting Presidential Assistance to Farmers and Fisher Folk (PAF), saying the crisis remained ongoing and many farmers and fisherfolk had benefited from the prior program. Chair Rep. Mikaela Angela B. Suansing said the PHP 10 billion provided in the 2026 General Appropriations Act had helped roughly 4.3 million farmers nationwide. Speaking on behalf of House Speaker Faustino Michael Carlos T. Dy III, she moved to provide PHP 10 billion for PAF under the Department of Agriculture (DA) in the 2027 budget. Rep. Wilfrido Mark M. Enverga seconded the motion, which Suansing declared carried without objection. Rep. Brian Poe supported maintaining the amendments through the budget process, citing complaints from farmers and fisherfolk in Pangasinan about inefficient fuel subsidies and fertilizer costs.

### PAF access, eligibility and delivery

Deputy Speaker Yevgeny Vincente B. Emano asked the committee to consider a provision making PAF accessible to farm workers. He said some farmers cultivate only 500 to 1,000 square meters while requirements may be one or two hectares, and described cases where assistance went to the landowner rather than the worker who tilled the land. Suansing said DA guidelines limited beneficiaries to Registry System for Basic Sectors in Agriculture (RSBSA) members and that a prior distribution had covered farms of two hectares and below. She agreed to discuss revising the guidelines with DA so more people could qualify. Enverga supported adding FishR registry members, noting that the prior fisherfolk list included BoatR registrants; Suansing said eligibility was then limited to RSBSA and BoatR and directed the Secretariat to note a possible PAF special provision, subject to coordination with DA and revised guidelines. These were proposed access changes and a direction to record a possible provision, not a separately carried motion.

Hernandez raised delays in transferring funds from DA to the Bureau of Fisheries and Aquatic Resources (BFAR), with some FISH-R beneficiaries still waiting, and urged faster delivery. Vice Chair Rep. Arnie B. Fuentebella called for corrected DA data. He said the prior target of PHP 10,000 per farmer had fallen to approximately PHP 2,325 as coverage widened; Suansing asked DA to update RSBSA records, remove duplicates and fix cases where farmers were registered under both rice and vegetable categories.

### Requested special provisions (not approved)

The Department of Agriculture requested authority to use its Quick Response Fund to acquire equipment directly needed for disaster response, mitigation, and recovery; restoration of the 2026 GAA provision for presidential assistance to farmers and fisherfolk in relation to an added amount in the BARSC recommendations; and PHP 51 million for citizen monitoring of DA-related programs, activities, and projects through its Philippine Council for Agriculture and Fisheries.

A separate support-for-animal-industry provision was described as substantive. The Committee Secretary recommended further consideration; no final disposition is recorded here.

## Department of Agrarian Reform (DAR) amendments

### Climate Resilience Farm Productivity Support

Deputy Speaker Janette L. Garin proposed a PHP 331 million increase for the Department of Agrarian Reform (DAR) under its Climate Resilience Farm Productivity Support program. She said most of the proposed funding would support farm-to-market roads serving agrarian reform communities, helping reduce transport costs worsened by high fuel prices. The chair restated the PHP 331 million addition for DAR, and the motion was seconded and carried without objection.

## Department of Environment and Natural Resources (DENR) amendments

### Land Management and Governance

While Rep. Albert S. Garcia was presiding, Deputy Speaker Jose C. Alvarez moved to increase DENR funding for Land Management and Governance by PHP 59,906,000 as part of proposals for inclusion in House Bill No. 10858. The motion was seconded and Garcia approved it.

### Release of unclaimed land patents

Alvarez said about 40,000 land patents remained unclaimed because owners could not afford the PHP 2,500 government fee per title. He moved to include PHP 100 million in the DENR budget to release the patents. Garcia approved the seconded motion without objection.

[01:54:08 · asr-002332](https://tordecilla.github.io/budget-hearings-2026/records/90/#asr-002332) · [01:54:21 · asr-002333](https://tordecilla.github.io/budget-hearings-2026/records/90/#asr-002333) · [01:54:30 · asr-002334](https://tordecilla.github.io/budget-hearings-2026/records/90/#asr-002334) · [01:54:40 · asr-002335](https://tordecilla.github.io/budget-hearings-2026/records/90/#asr-002335) · [01:54:42 · asr-002336](https://tordecilla.github.io/budget-hearings-2026/records/90/#asr-002336) · [01:54:43 · asr-002337](https://tordecilla.github.io/budget-hearings-2026/records/90/#asr-002337)

### Region 10 program start for structures

For the final DENR item, Alvarez said a few structures in Region 10 could not receive a large lump sum under fiscal constraints. He moved to add PHP 50 million to start the program there. Garcia approved the seconded motion without objection.

[01:54:51 · asr-002340](https://tordecilla.github.io/budget-hearings-2026/records/90/#asr-002340) · [01:55:09 · asr-002341](https://tordecilla.github.io/budget-hearings-2026/records/90/#asr-002341) · [01:55:11 · asr-002342](https://tordecilla.github.io/budget-hearings-2026/records/90/#asr-002342)

### Requested special provisions (not approved)

The Department of Environment and Natural Resources requested a new Climate Change Information Management System provision to strengthen its network as integrated climate infrastructure, and a provision requiring nature-based approaches where feasible. It also requested a new interagency water-infrastructure warning and forecasting mandate.

Other proposed replacements would require regular assessment and protection of vulnerable habitats and ecosystems and establish joint DENR-DPWH development and maintenance of a river-basin and flood-management database. Additional requests covered quality-assured sector greenhouse-gas inventories and prompt land registration following patent issuance.

A new provision for mineral-reservation and critical-minerals revenues would automatically release the mineral-reservation appropriation and the Mines and Geosciences Bureau 10% share.

### Restoration and strengthening of public-service media capacity

Yamsuan moved a PHP 600 million increase for the People's Television Network, Inc. to restore and strengthen its capacity as an active, credible, multi-platform public-service media network. He said the funding would improve content and nationwide reach, develop marketable programming, expand its audience and revenue-generating capacity, and deliver reliable public-service information on additional digital platforms. Garcia put the PHP 600 million motion to the body; it was seconded and approved without objection, and he said government stations should be upgraded.

## Energy Regulatory Commission (ERC) amendments

### Original ERC request and refinement

Rep. Jurdin Jesus "JJ" M. Romualdo moved to recommend an ERC amendment in the proposed text budget for House Bill 10858. He first stated PHP 229 million, corrected the total through PHP 227 million and PHP 220 million, then settled on PHP 227 million for consultants addressing the backlog of regulatory resets involving distribution utilities, capital expenditure plans and generation companies, especially system-loss issues. Rep. Albert S. Garcia put the PHP 227 million motion to the body; it was seconded and approved without objection.

Rep. Arnie B. Fuentebella urged the committee to provide the full amount, citing ERC's oversight of a trillion-peso industry, its technical workload and a 71.3 billion refund dated August 3, 2026. He discussed the backlog of resets extending from 2012 toward 2026 and the need to review utility, transmission and generation-company costs. His remarks supported Romualdo's request; they were not a separate motion.

Romualdo later withdrew his earlier motion and appealed to set aside PHP 25 million each for work involving distribution utilities, capital expenditure plans and generation companies, a stated PHP 75 million total if funds remained available. Garcia asked him to clarify the motion; no separate vote on this conditional set-aside is recorded before Fuentebella presented specific insertion lines.

## Department of the Interior and Local Government (DILG) amendments

### NAPOLCOM independent police oversight

For the Department of the Interior and Local Government (DILG), Rep. Arnie B. Fuentebella moved PHP 10 million for the National Police Commission (NAPOLCOM) to strengthen independent police oversight through confidential and intelligence funds. He cited the commission's handling of 4,000 cases and a 25-year backlog that had been reduced to zero, and described its role in disciplining and policing the police. Fuentebella renewed the PHP 10 million motion; Rep. Albert S. Garcia put it to the body, and it was seconded and approved without objection.

### PNP Counterterrorism Center

For the Philippine National Police (PNP), Fuentebella moved to establish the PNP Counterterrorism Center, a combined operations and data facility for cybercrime and cybersecurity work. He said the proposal was PHP 500 million and that he hoped the committee could provide PHP 200 million; with limited fiscal space, he initially moved PHP 100 million.

Garcia asked whether the project was shovel-ready. Fuentebella said the design and structure were complete and renewed his PHP 200 million request, emphasizing the cost of the IT component. He later said the committee could begin with PHP 100 million, citing the need for the facility and citing more than 40 people saved by police while discussing incidents involving child deaths. He described the project as a composite team involving DSWD, PNP, DILG and DepEd. Garcia put the PHP 100 million amount to a motion, Rep. Brian Poe seconded, and it was approved without objection.

## Department of Transportation (DOTr) amendments

### Service Contracting Program

Rep. Edwin Ongchuan moved PHP 500 million for the Service Contracting Program (SCP), noting that it had zero funding in the 2027 NEP and citing its value to constituents amid unstable fuel prices and the Middle East crisis. He said Secretary Giovanni Lopez's agency request was PHP 5 billion. The motion was seconded and approved without objection. Garcia recalled the House contingent proposing PHP 1 billion for the program during 2026 bicameral deliberations, providing funding the Executive could draw on when the crisis broke out. Ongchuan also cited Rep. Percival "Perci" Cendaña's support for the program.

## National Poverty Alleviation Commission (NAPC) amendments

### NAPC MOOE appropriation

Minority Leader Marcelino C. Libanan said he had received a letter from NAPC Chairman Manny Pacquiao, who had just been sworn in. Libanan said NAPC had PHP 359 million for personal services and maintenance and operating expenses. Pacquiao's letter requested PHP 10 billion for the Masaganang Pamumuhay sa Bagong Pilipinas poverty-reduction program for local government units. He sought further funding if fiscal space could be found before bicameral deliberations. With only PHP 100 million available in current fiscal space, Libanan moved to appropriate PHP 100 million for the program, then clarified that the immediate appropriation would be general NAPC maintenance and operating expenses rather than a program-specific allocation. The chair said the motion was duly seconded, heard no objections, and approved it; he later showed PHP 100 million MOOE for NAPC.

## Local Water Utilities Administration (LWUA) amendments

### Water supply augmentation

Deputy Speaker Janette L. Garin moved to add PHP 100 million to the Local Water Utilities Administration (LWUA), whose FY2027 NEP allocation she identified as PHP 29.55 million. She said the request covered areas with water problems; when Deputy Speaker Jose C. Alvarez asked where the money would go, Garin said she did not have the exact list with her, but that it had been vetted and was with the Secretariat.

Alvarez said the increase would be insufficient for the incoming El Nino and, as a manifestation rather than a motion, urged a further increase if fiscal space remained after deliberations. Garin agreed that any augmentation would be welcome. The committee seconded Garin's motion and approved the additional PHP 100 million without objection; the chair later restated it as additional LWUA funding.

## Department of Social Welfare and Development (DSWD) amendments

### Assistance to Individuals in Crisis Situations

Rep. JC Rahman A. Nava, M.D., presenting a wish list from DSWD Secretary Rex Gatchalian, said Assistance to Individuals in Crisis Situations (AICS) had a FY2027 NEP allocation of PHP 33 billion, compared with more than PHP 63 billion in the FY2026 GAA. He said the agency sought PHP 76 billion, a PHP 43 billion increase, but proposed an additional PHP 25.806 billion because of limited fiscal space, and moved to increase the AICS allocation by that amount. The BARSC proposal column displayed the AICS addition as PHP 25,806,000,000. The motion was seconded and approved without objection.

Minority Leader Marcelino C. Libanan seconded and supported the motion, but cited PHP 29.806 billion and said it would bring the program to PHP 59 billion, still PHP 4.8 billion below a FY2026 appropriation he described as PHP 6.3 billion. Libanan's figures do not reconcile with Nava's proposal and the stated NEP; the motion is reported as an additional appropriation, without inferring a resulting allocation.

### Quick Response Fund ceiling within the NDRRMF

Nava moved to raise the Quick Response Fund ceiling from PHP 1.25 billion to PHP 3 billion, an additional PHP 1.75 billion in ceiling, following the ceiling increase under the FY2026 GAA. The motion was seconded and approved without objection.

Chair Mikaela Angela B. Suansing clarified that this was not an additional allocation or a reduction in the overall fund: it was a reallocation from the National Disaster Risk Reduction and Management Fund (NDRRMF) to the Quick Response Fund. She said Secretary Rex Gatchalian requested it because replenishment could take several weeks to several months after the QRF reached 50 percent of its ceiling.

### Northern Mindanao Disaster Resource and Support Center

Nava requested a Northern Mindanao Disaster Resource and Support Center in Manolo Fortich, Bukidnon, as a forward support facility to speed relief-goods distribution. Deputy Speaker Yevgeny Vincente B. Emano supported a Mindanao center in Bukidnon, citing its central location and transport constraints: a C-130 could carry 6,000 packs, a vessel 60,000, while the region needed at least 3 million food packs in stock. Deputy Speaker Jose C. Alvarez commented that Maramag was in central Mindanao; this did not revise Nava's proposed Manolo Fortich site. Nava said PHP 100 million would be allocated to establish the project; the motion was seconded and approved without objection.

Suansing cautioned that PHP 500 million allocated the previous year for the first disaster resource and support site in Caraga had not been released, and appealed to DSWD and the Department of Budget and Management (DBM) for the release, expressing concern that the same delay might affect the second site.

## Department of Health (DOH) amendments

### MAIFIP increase and remaining funding gap

As DOH budget sponsor, Rep. Albert S. Garcia moved an additional PHP 24,965,776,000 for the Medical Assistance to Indigent and Financially Incapacitated Patients (MAIFIP) program, whose FY 2027 NEP allocation was PHP 24,235,078,000. Garcia said the resulting program total would be around PHP 49,203,854,000. Chair Mikaela Angela B. Suansing first said the increase would restore the 2026 GAA level, then corrected that after the motion carried: it would restore the 2026 H-GAB level, leaving a shortfall of around PHP 2 billion. Minority Leader Marcelino C. Libanan had earlier estimated a PHP 2.6 billion gap. The motion was seconded and carried without objection. Deputy Speaker Yevgeny Vincente B. Emano cited a DOH measure that might compel doctors to accept MAIFIP professional-fee payments and urged further increases if funds become available. Garcia agreed that the program should be augmented if fiscal space permits.

[03:21:44 · asr-004336](https://tordecilla.github.io/budget-hearings-2026/records/90/#asr-004336) · [03:23:14 · asr-004345](https://tordecilla.github.io/budget-hearings-2026/records/90/#asr-004345) · [03:24:01 · asr-004352](https://tordecilla.github.io/budget-hearings-2026/records/90/#asr-004352) · [03:24:17 · asr-004356](https://tordecilla.github.io/budget-hearings-2026/records/90/#asr-004356) · [03:24:28 · asr-004362](https://tordecilla.github.io/budget-hearings-2026/records/90/#asr-004362) · [03:26:25 · asr-004406](https://tordecilla.github.io/budget-hearings-2026/records/90/#asr-004406) · [03:35:09 · asr-004613](https://tordecilla.github.io/budget-hearings-2026/records/90/#asr-004613) · [03:36:00 · asr-004631](https://tordecilla.github.io/budget-hearings-2026/records/90/#asr-004631) · [03:36:12 · asr-004635](https://tordecilla.github.io/budget-hearings-2026/records/90/#asr-004635) · [03:36:18 · asr-004637](https://tordecilla.github.io/budget-hearings-2026/records/90/#asr-004637)

### MAIFIP access and proposed special-provision wording

Garcia described MAIFIP as assistance for patients who cannot meet hospital bills and professional fees, supporting zero-balanced billing in government hospitals and help for patients sent to private hospitals. He said DOH Secretary Edwin M. Mercado and Philippine Health Insurance Corporation (PhilHealth) President and CEO Dr. Beverly Lorraine Ho planned to work with hospitals and medical societies on an opt-in arrangement in which hospitals would agree by memorandum of agreement to include professional fees as well as hospital bills. Suansing said the GAA already authorizes MAIFIP to cover up to 100 percent of professional fees, but implementation has a gap.

Deputy Speaker Janette L. Garin asked for a special provision that would make access less restrictive and reduce documentation. She said her submitted letter proposed coverage for indigent patients, financially incapacitated patients, and patients whose out-of-pocket spending is more than 60 percent; while describing the program's current implementation, she also said the third category is patients at or above 60 percent. Garin sought flexibility for patients who need a regular private room, including patients with active tuberculosis who could not use a ward, while excluding suite rooms. She said the original Medical Assistance Program approach would require fewer documents and promised to send her proposed text after she returned home. Rep. JC Rahman A. Nava, M.D., described regular private-room needs for immunocompromised or highly infectious patients; Garin explained the existing protocol's broader categories.

Rep. Brian Raymund S. Yamsuan, Rep. Arnie B. Fuentebella, Senior Deputy Speaker Ferdinand L. Hernandez, Rep. Brian Poe and Libanan supported broader access for middle-income and near-poor households facing large medical bills and difficult indigency documentation. Suansing invited members to help refine the wording, and Garcia said the committee looked forward to the proposal. The special provision remained a proposal in this exchange; no vote on its wording is recorded. Garcia also described the longer-term plan to merge the assistance with PhilHealth coverage to institutionalize support and reduce out-of-pocket costs.

### Health Facilities Enhancement Program funding increase

Garcia moved an additional PHP 1,549,000,000 for the Department of Health's Health Facilities Enhancement Program (HFEP), on top of the PHP 4,472,659,000 in the FY 2027 proposed budget. The motion was seconded and carried without objection. The final BARSC proposal spreadsheet also displays PHP 1,549,000,000 for HFEP.

Chair Mikaela Angela B. Suansing urged DOH and DBM to accelerate HFEP implementation, citing accumulated unutilized funds and a backlog of 2025 and 2026 projects. She asked DOH to prepare a catch-up plan.

### Proposed reconciliation of PhilHealth balances for special-provision access

Garcia said the special provision he was preparing would also address PhilHealth: he proposed reconciling its balances and unallocated funds derived from sin taxes and other sources, including the Philippine Charity Sweepstakes Office (PCSO) and Philippine Amusement and Gaming Corporation (PAGCOR). He said the Bureau of the Treasury, Department of Budget and Management (DBM), and DOH would reconcile the amounts; if a balance remained, they would include access to it in the special provision. Garcia linked the approach to Suansing's earlier resolution of State Universities and Colleges arrears in the 2026 budget. This provision was still being developed and was conditional on identifying balances.

[03:46:11 · asr-004852](https://tordecilla.github.io/budget-hearings-2026/records/90/#asr-004852) · [03:46:21 · asr-004855](https://tordecilla.github.io/budget-hearings-2026/records/90/#asr-004855) · [03:46:25 · asr-004857](https://tordecilla.github.io/budget-hearings-2026/records/90/#asr-004857) · [03:46:30 · asr-004859](https://tordecilla.github.io/budget-hearings-2026/records/90/#asr-004859) · [03:46:32 · asr-004860](https://tordecilla.github.io/budget-hearings-2026/records/90/#asr-004860) · [03:46:33 · asr-004861](https://tordecilla.github.io/budget-hearings-2026/records/90/#asr-004861) · [03:46:41 · asr-004864](https://tordecilla.github.io/budget-hearings-2026/records/90/#asr-004864) · [03:46:52 · asr-004869](https://tordecilla.github.io/budget-hearings-2026/records/90/#asr-004869)

### Requested special provisions (not approved)

The Department of Health requested raising the engineering and administrative overhead ceiling for Health Facilities Enhancement Program projects over PHP 1 million from 1% to 2.5%. Other requested provisions covered medical assistance for indigent and financially incapacitated patients; health-workforce management and development; augmentation of operating requirements for DOH hospitals and other facilities; direct release of research funds to PIDS; and honoraria for non-DOH members of special technical and advisory boards. The readout attributed these items to Acting Secretary Edwin Mercado.

Two requests sought a subsidy for the National Health Insurance Program: one attributed to Representative Percival "Perci" Cendaña and another to Secretary Edwin Mercado. A separate new provision would address treatment of reorganization expenditures. A further release-of-funds request called for DOH and PhilHealth to coordinate the amounts for nationally procured commodities.

### Project-detail correction provision and safeguards

On Momo's proposed provision for correcting errors in attached project details, Deputy Speaker Jose C. Alvarez recommended saying "correct" rather than "modify", warning that broad modification without an error could appear suspect; Momo agreed. Suansing said modification is the technical term in the General Provisions, while framing the change as correction of wrong entries, coordinates and misprints. She said proposed DPWH items had the five required documentary supports: BP 202, geotagging, an implementability and feasibility certificate, the program of work, and development-council approval (RDC or MDC, as applicable); she said projects for the House General Appropriations Bill had been vetted under DBM and DPWH requirements.

Brian Poe confirmed that the provision would permit correction of errors, station numbers, coordinates, wording or other entries, but not alter amounts; Suansing and Momo confirmed that amounts remain unchanged. Suansing proposed extending the approach to Department of Agriculture farm-to-market roads, National Irrigation Administration projects, irrigation works and classrooms because errors and delays can waste funds and delay economic benefits. Momo agreed. The provision remained for refinement.

## Department of Labor and Employment (DOLE) amendments

### TUPAD emergency-employment increase

Rep. Johanne Monich G. Bautista said the agency had requested an increase of PHP 5 billion for Tulong Panghanap Buhay sa Ating Disadvantaged or Displaced Workers (TUPAD), from PHP 14,246,077,000 to PHP 19,246,077,000. She cited the program's emergency jobs for displaced, underemployed and seasonal workers, warning that an expected extreme El Nino could affect livelihoods. She also pointed to more than 17,000 transporters helped through TUPAD Tuloy Pasada during the energy emergency, when drivers stopped operating because they could not afford fuel. Chair Rep. Mikaela Angela B. Suansing said the motion was seconded; with no objection, she declared it carried.

## Local Government Support Fund (LGSF) amendments

### Local-government support and project discretion

Deputy Speaker Yevgeny Vincente B. Emano supported helping the Local Government Support Fund, referring to an earlier mention of local government units. Drawing on his local-government experience, he said barangays had authority to implement projects of their choosing based on constituents' needs, and that mayors and governors also had opportunities to implement needed projects. He hoped the committee could later find ways to help and support the fund, saying this could benefit local government and the country.

### LGSF increase motion

Deputy Speaker Yevgeny Vincente B. Emano moved to increase the Local Government Support Fund (LGSF), arguing that additional support would let barangays, municipalities, cities and provinces implement projects they choose for local needs. As illustrative tiers he hoped to follow, he named PHP 5 million for barangay soft projects, PHP 10 million for hard projects, PHP 20 million for municipalities, and PHP 50 million each for cities and provinces; these were examples, not separate motions. Emano recited the NEP figure as '58 billion, 532,578'; the proposal sheet displayed the LGSF's 2027 NEP at PHP 58,532,578,000 and his BARSC proposal at PHP 4,091,400,000. Suansing summarized the proposed increase as PHP 4.09 billion, and the motion was seconded.

## Department of National Defense (DND) amendments

### DND amendment particulars withheld for national security

Before DND's amendments, Deputy Speaker Yevgeny Vincente B. Emano asked whether the request concerned national security. After a brief suspension, Brian Poe proposed identifying the requests by amount while withholding particulars because many were classified. He said DND's actual request was available and that stating amounts would serve transparency. The body accepted that approach, and Aquino presented numbered allocations without item descriptions.

## Department of Migrant Workers (DMW) amendments

### AKSYON Fund for migrant-worker assistance

Rep. Johanne Monich G. Bautista said the AKSYON Fund was also agency-requested and that DMW was seeking another PHP 1 billion for it. She said the FY 2027 NEP level was PHP 2 billion, the same amount provided the previous year. With fiscal space limited, she moved for an increase of at least PHP 150 million; Suansing formally put a PHP 150 million increase, and it was carried without objection.

Suansing recalled, with uncertainty, that DMW and the Overseas Workers Welfare Administration (OWWA) had received about PHP 4 billion from UPLIFT and asked whether it had been used. Bautista said she believed the 2026 funding had been fully used and a supplemental budget was provided during the Middle East crisis, but she was unsure whether the supplemental amount had since been utilized and suggested obtaining the data.

Bautista explained that the AKSYON Fund assists overseas Filipino workers and their families with legal and financial help, repatriation, housing and shelter, including workers who lose jobs abroad.

### OWWA Emergency Repatriation Fund increase

Bautista said the FY 2027 NEP set the OWWA Emergency Repatriation Fund (ERF) at PHP 997 million after a reduction of several hundred million. She described the prior-year amount uncertainly as "1.2, 1.3 billion"; Suansing said OWWA's primary request was restoration to its FY 2026 GAA level, and Bautista clarified that the reduction was more than PHP 200 million. The agency had requested an additional PHP 575 million from the NEP level. Bautista reported rapid use of the fund during the Middle East crisis and a supplemental budget.

Given limited fiscal space, Bautista moved for at least PHP 100 million. After Suansing asked whether the motion was for at least or exactly that amount, Suansing put a fixed PHP 100 million increase, and it carried without objection. Bautista later repeated the agency's PHP 575 million ERF request for further consideration; that request was not a second adopted motion.

## Amendment procedure and outcomes

### Scope and authority of the 2027 amendment review

Chair Rep. Mikaela Angela B. Suansing said the Budget Amendments Review Subcommittee (BARSC) was established to make budget review more transparent, broaden public participation and align amendments with urgent public needs. In its second consecutive year, the BARSC would deliberate on amendments to the proposed 2027 National Budget arising from agency requests. Only institutional amendments submitted by agencies and signed by the agency head would be considered. The BARSC would also take account of recommendations from Committee on Appropriations deliberations and the two weeks of Plenary deliberations. Its actions are recommendations: the House and Plenary retain authority to act on the proposed amendments and recommendations.

[00:04:22 · asr-000026](https://tordecilla.github.io/budget-hearings-2026/records/90/#asr-000026) · [00:05:00 · asr-000034](https://tordecilla.github.io/budget-hearings-2026/records/90/#asr-000034) · [00:05:11 · asr-000036](https://tordecilla.github.io/budget-hearings-2026/records/90/#asr-000036) · [00:05:24 · asr-000039](https://tordecilla.github.io/budget-hearings-2026/records/90/#asr-000039) · [00:05:37 · asr-000041](https://tordecilla.github.io/budget-hearings-2026/records/90/#asr-000041) · [00:05:59 · asr-000046](https://tordecilla.github.io/budget-hearings-2026/records/90/#asr-000046) · [00:06:05 · asr-000047](https://tordecilla.github.io/budget-hearings-2026/records/90/#asr-000047)

### Public, representative, line-by-line review

Suansing said the process replaced the small committee with live-streamed, line-by-line deliberations. Members from the parties, party-list coalition and minority were represented through party nominations; the minority leader represented the minority. Members had received the agencies' voluminous requests in advance to review and select items for consideration. A BARSC member had to raise or nominate an amendment before it could be considered. The subcommittee would deliberate by department and decide each line item separately, choosing whether to recommend adoption, revision or deferral. Suansing framed the process as a commitment to transparent, accountable and people-centered budget deliberation.

## Amendment procedure and outcomes

### Fiscal space and review criteria

Senior Deputy Speaker Ferdinand L. Hernandez contrasted last year's BARSC, when he said a ready source of 250 billion had been taken from flood control, with this year's very limited fiscal space. He said the committee would have to look for sources to accommodate departmental requests and review departments for slow-moving items, absorptive capacity and low utilization. He described this year's exercise as harder because funds available to increase support for agencies were limited. His reference to 250 billion was a prior-year source, not a 2027 amendment total.

Despite the thousands of line items already under review, agencies continued to submit last-minute requests.

### Limited fiscal space and unfunded or underfunded priorities

Suansing said last year PHP 255 billion was taken from Department of Public Works and Highways (DPWH) flood-control projects and reallocated across agencies. This year, she said, there was no comparable readily available source, so the BARSC had to examine how to fund important programs within limited fiscal space. She cited the ARAL program under the Department of Education (DepEd) as having zero budget in the National Expenditure Program (NEP), and said school security funding was absent from the NEP. She also pointed to service contracting and the fuel transport subsidy among programs described as unfunded or underfunded in the NEP. She said the BARSC had heard these needs and would review how to fund them; as she put it, funding one program requires taking from another.

Garin said BARSC members had received additional agency requests in view of the upcoming Super El Nino and its expected impact on the Philippines and Filipino families, as well as the continuing global conflict. Her manifestation raised those requests and their context for the committee's deliberations.

### Civil-society and local-government recommendations

Representative Albert S. Garcia urged the BARSC to consider civil-society concerns and alternative proposals alongside agency requests, in light of limited fiscal space and geopolitical and climate pressures. He said proposals came through appropriations vice chairs, directly to the Chair, and through local-government representatives to the Chair or Committee on Appropriations. He said the BARSC was considering these recommendations with agency requests to fit priority national-budget needs within the available fiscal space. Chair Mikaela Angela B. Suansing assured civil-society partners that they would have a voice in deliberations; she said Garcia and appropriations vice chairs who had engaged the groups would help guide review of their proposals.

[00:16:21 · asr-000360](https://tordecilla.github.io/budget-hearings-2026/records/90/#asr-000360) · [00:16:42 · asr-000370](https://tordecilla.github.io/budget-hearings-2026/records/90/#asr-000370) · [00:17:19 · asr-000386](https://tordecilla.github.io/budget-hearings-2026/records/90/#asr-000386) · [00:17:31 · asr-000392](https://tordecilla.github.io/budget-hearings-2026/records/90/#asr-000392) · [00:18:14 · asr-000399](https://tordecilla.github.io/budget-hearings-2026/records/90/#asr-000399)

### Minority Leader asks about limited fiscal space

Minority Leader Marcelino C. Libanan asked why fiscal space remained limited despite a larger budget. He cited 6.78 trillion allocated in the 2026 budget, 7.2 trillion now, and characterized the increase as 6%, then asked for an explanation. Chair Mikaela Angela B. Suansing said the budget had risen from 6.8 trillion to 7.2 trillion pesos, which in theory made 400 billion more available for allocation. She explained that mandatory appropriations absorbed the increase: the new Salary Standardization Law tranche, an increase in the subsistence allowance under the MUP, interest payments, a new Department of Agriculture law she called ANSEF (20 billion), an increase in the National Tax Allocation, and 12 billion for the judiciary fiscal autonomy law. She said she could not break down the full 400 billion, but after netting mandatory allocations there was no increase in programmed appropriations the BARSC could move within the House General Appropriations Bill (HGAB).

Representative Brian Raymund S. Yamsuan said agencies had sent requests for special provisions in addition to specific project requests, to address implementation needs and prevent bottlenecks. ### Line-by-line transparency and member floor

Chair Mikaela Angela B. Suansing said the House's commitment was to show the public, line by line, the amendments to be introduced into the 2027 budget, as part of transparent and accountable budget-making. She opened the floor for BARSC members to make manifestations and asked whether anyone else wished to speak before the substantive discussion began.

### Agency-by-agency review

Chairperson Rep. Mikaela Angela B. Suansing proposed using a free-flowing discussion in which BARSC members would choose the agency to take up. She suggested beginning with the education cluster because of its constitutional budgetary share, then starting with the Department of Education (DepEd). She later explained that the committee would display amendments line by line, including the NEP level, proposed amount, and new total for each program, and would proceed with line-by-line approval after conferring with BARSC.

### Approved amendments routed through the substitute bill

Rep. Albert S. Garcia moved that the amendments already approved by the Budget Amendments Review Subcommittee be incorporated into the proposed substitute text of House Bill No. 10858 under Committee Report 638, for the House Committee on Appropriations to submit to the Plenary for consideration. After the motion was seconded, Chair Mikaela Angela B. Suansing restated its terms, called for objections, and declared it carried when none was raised.

Suansing clarified that the subcommittee would issue no separate report: its line-by-line approved output would be incorporated in the Appropriations Committee substitute bill and could be reviewed there once that bill was filed for Plenary consideration.
